I’ll just sit at the kitchen table, staring out at the yard, and it feels like… I don’t know, like there isn’t really a good version of the future coming.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes. I used to make time for things like hiking, yoga, working in my garden, taking bird photos… and lately I just don’t. I’ll cancel plans or leave my camera sitting in the closet because none of it feels worth the effort.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this way most of the time for at least two years?\n\nPatient: No, not for two years. It’s been more like the last couple of months—since we took on the mortgage. Before this low stretch, I was in this really overcharged push around business stuff, and then it swung down into how I’m feeling now.\n\nDoctor: During that overcharged period, did you actually feel unusually up or energized compared with your normal self?\n\nPatient: Yes. In the first few weeks after we signed the mortgage papers, I felt almost lit up—like I was unstoppable. But it wasn’t just happy energy. I’d also get really irritable, like snapping at my husband or coworkers over tiny delays that normally wouldn’t bother me.\n\nDoctor: During that overcharged period, did you need much less sleep than usual and still feel full of energy?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I know I felt wired and driven, but I don’t remember clearly whether I was actually sleeping a lot less and still feeling fine.\n\nDoctor: During that overcharged period, were you getting much more done than usual or taking on a lot more projects than you normally would?\n\nPatient: Yes, definitely. I was doing way more than usual. I started making new research templates, writing up pitches, and reaching out to potential clients after hours—like I was ready to launch the business right then, even though the office was still basically an unfinished outbuilding.\n\nDoctor: During that overcharged period, were you talking more than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I don’t remember anyone saying I was talking nonstop or anything like that. I may have been more excited when I talked about plans, but I can’t say for sure that I was talking more than usual.\n\nDoctor: During that overcharged period, did you feel unusually confident or powerful compared with your normal self?\n\nPatient: Yes. I felt way more confident than I normally do. I really believed I could grow past my current firm pretty quickly and build my own consulting business better than the people around me, even though I didn’t actually have clients lined up yet.\n\nDoctor: During that overcharged period, did you feel unusually driven to start or pursue lots of projects or plans compared with your normal self?\n\nPatient: Yes. I was much more up and moving than usual. I couldn’t really sit still—I'd keep jumping up to sketch out office layouts, reorganize files, or start another task even when there wasn’t any real reason it had to be done right then.\n\nDoctor: During that overcharged period, were you more active than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes, definitely.
